| Conclusion | The following is RAN1’s understanding for Case 1 in Question 1 in the RAN2 LS on per band and per BC capability (R1-2506724):<br>- When UE indicates both per band and per BC capability, regardless of whether CA is configured or not, if the capability/component is not counted across CCs, then the minimum capability between per BC capability and per band capability should be applied for a band in case of band combination (CA)<br>  - RAN1 will continue to discuss the case(s) when the capability is counted across CCs<br>- RAN1 will review by RAN1 #123 for which FGs special rules/handling may need to be defined in Rel. 19, e.g., in some cases a clear “ranking” between capabilities may need to be clarified in the Notes | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2508081](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2508081.zip) |
| Conclusion | The following is RAN1’s understanding for Case 2 in Question 1 in the RAN2 LS on per band and per BC capability (R1-2506724):<br>- RAN2’s understanding is generally correct<br>- RAN1 will review by RAN1 #123 for which FGs special rules/handling may need to be defined in Rel. 19 | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2508081](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2508081.zip) |
| Conclusion | The following is RAN1’s understanding for Case 3 in Question 1 in the RAN2 LS on per band and per BC capability (R1-2506724):<br>- RAN2’s understanding is generally correct<br>- RAN1 will review by RAN1 #123 for which FGs special rules/handling may need to be defined in Rel. 19 | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2508081](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2508081.zip) |
| Conclusion | The following is RAN1’s understanding for Question 2 in the RAN2 LS on per band and per BC capability (R1-2506724):<br>- For per band and per BC capabilities, it is expected that UE indicates the same granularity as the pre-requisite | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2508081](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2508081.zip) |

| Agreement | Support precoding matrix with port-subband domain representation as target CSI type.<br>- For a certain layer , a precoding matrix on N_sb subband and P CSI-RS ports is  with  being a  vector.<br>- Note: Only sub-band PMI reporting is supported. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2508021](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2508021.zip) |
| Agreement | For determining the payload parameters combinations  or   across all layers and ranks, consider following general principles for precoding matrix-based target CSI<br>- Rank-common and layer-common model with payload-scalable design is considered as the baseline implementation for discussion purpose.<br>  - Note: layer-specific / rank-specific (if supported) payload parameters are supported by this rank-common and layer-common model with payload-scalable design.<br>- Limiting the total number of (per-layer) payload parameters combinations  or   across all layers and all ranks.<br>- At least for medium to high per-layer payload regime, the total payload size of rank 2, rank 3 and 4 are comparable to each other.<br>- Spectral efficiency or system throughput and trade-off with feedback overhead are considered as the performance metric. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2508021](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2508021.zip) |
| Agreement | When the Target CSI is precoder matrix, in order to down-select to N per-layer payload parameter combinations (PC) of  or  based on performance, overhead and complexity trade-off results, consider the following candidate values:<br>- Candiate values for : 32, 64, 96, 128, 192<br>- Candidate values for : 1, 2, 3, 4, (8, 10 only for VQ)<br>  - 2 is considered as the baseline assumption for SQ.<br>- Candidate values for L: L=2, 4,<br>- Candidate values for N: 4, 5, 6<br>- Note: These N payload PCs of  or  are considered as the pool for the payload PC for each layer and each rank.<br>- Note: The above is for discussion purpose, how to configure the PCs (across layers and ranks) is a separate discussion.<br>- Note: Rank-common and layer-common model with payload scalable design is considered.<br>- Note: The above does not mean support of both SQ and VQ.<br>- Note: The number of parameters combinations configurations (Y) does not exceed 8. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2508022](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2508022.zip) |
| Agreement | For payload parameter combinations (PC), consider followings<br>- Support Y configurable PCs (across layers and ranks) as below, where X_n, Xn_31, Xn_32, Xn_33, Xn_41, Xn_42, Xn_43, Xn_44 represent a pair of  or a triplet of   chosen from the per-layer PC pool.<br>\|  \| Rank1 \| Rank2 \| Rank3 \| Rank4 \|<br>\| PC1 \| X1 \| X1, X1 \| X1_31, X1_32, X1_33 \| X1_41, X1_42, X1_43, X1_44 \|<br>\| PC2 \| X2 \| X2, X2 \| X2_31, X2_32, X2_33 \| X2_41, X2_42, X2_43, X2_44 \|<br>\| … \|  \|  \|  \|  \|<br>- For rank 1 and rank 2, layer-common and rank-common payload is supported.<br>- For rank 3, consider alternatives of {Xn_31, Xn_32, Xn_33} = {a, a, a}, {a, a, b}, {a, b, b} for potential down selection.<br>- For rank 4, consider alternatives of {Xn_41, Xn_42, Xn_43, Xn_44} = {a, a, a, a}, {a, a, b, b}, {a, b, b, c}, {a, a, b, c} or {a, b, c, c} for potential down selection.<br>- Note: The table is for discussion purpose, how to configure the PCs (across layers and ranks) is a separate discussion.<br>- Note: a, b values in rank3 can be different from that in rank 4. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2508022](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2508022.zip) |

| Agreement | For NW side data collection with higher layer reporting, for further study of Option 1 (scalar quantization), consider the following definition for evaluation:<br>- Content: for a number of S=1 (as baseline) Target CSI samples each with the dimensions of 2N1N2 ports, N3 subbands, v layers for precoding matrix, and real+imaginary (or amplitude+phase) parts, which has overall 2N1N2*N3*v complex elements, and any complex element has a real (or amplitude) part of x1 and an imaginary (or phase) part of x2.<br>  - Note: Companies to report whether  real value and imaginary value are quantized, or amplitude value and phase value are quantized.<br>- Format: x1 is quantized to k1 bits within range of [T1min, T1max], and x2 is quantized to k2 bits within range of [T2min, T2max]. E.g., for Scalar8, k1=8 bits for quantizing the real part, and k2=8 bits for quantizing the imaginary part. Uniform quantization is assumed.<br>  - Note: Companies to report whether/how the normalization is performed, and whether it is normalized within or across 2N1N2 ports/ N3 subbands/ v layers/ real and imaginary (or amplitude and phase) parts.<br>- Fixed parameter values of k1/k2/T1min/T1max/T2min/T2max across complex elements, as a baseline for evaluations. Companies to report the values.<br>- Note: It is up to company to use number of samples S>1 in their evaluations. In this case, companies need to provide information how S samples are quantized in their evaluation. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2508028](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2508028.zip) |

Reference amplitude = 4bits/6bits, differential amplitude = 3bits/4bits, and phase = 4bits/6bits<br>  - Company to report the quantization step for reference amplitude for 6bits.<br>- No restriction to the total number of non-zero coefficients for a specific rank.<br>- Other combinations can be considered and reported by companies. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2508030](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2508030.zip) |

| Agreement | For early triggering of aperiodic CSI reporting and the associated CSI-RS for CSI when UE transition from IDLE/INACTIVE to CONNECTED mode, support Option-2 as follows:<br>- In Step-1, SIBx provides one or multiple CSI report configurations based on one or multiple UE capability assumptions<br>  - Each CSI reporting configuration is associated with a CSI resource configuration for channel measurement.<br>    - The CSI-RS resource set(s) in the CSI resource configuration for channel measurement is provided with a same number of CSI-RS ports<br>  - Each CSI reporting configuration is associated with a CSI resource configuration for CSI-IM based interference measurement.<br>  - FFS: Support of NZP CSI-RS based interference measurement<br>  - FFS: Information to be provided in a CSI report/resource configuration<br>- In Step-2, UE reports through MSG3 which/whether the CSI report configuration(s) provided in SIBx is/are supported.<br>  - FFS: How to report which/whether the CSI report configuration(s) provided in SIBx is/are supported. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2506840](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2506840.zip) |
| Agreement | On PUSCH allocation for MSG4-triggered aperiodic CSI reporting associated with aperiodic CSI-RS for CSI, when UE transition from IDLE/INACTIVE to CONNECTED mode, down select one from the following alternatives to determine PUSCH for carrying the aperiodic CSI report by RAN1#123:<br>- Alt-1: The PUSCH is scheduled by MAC CE in MSG4 along with the aperiodic CSI report triggering.<br>- Alt-2: The PUSCH is scheduled by a legacy DCI after MSG4<br>- Alt-3: The PUSCH is configured by SIBx providing resource/reporting configuration for early triggering of aperiodic CSI reporting.<br>Note: The timeline of PUSCH for aperiodic CSI reporting is separately discussed. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2508115](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2508115.zip) |
| Agreement | On triggering mechanism for early aperiodic SRS-AS transmission on a SCell and early aperiodic CSI reporting for a SCell, based on the legacy SCell activation activating the SCell, down-select one from the followings in RAN1#123 meeting:<br>- Alt-1 (Implicit mechanism):<br>  - For early aperiodic SRS-AS transmission, the SRS resource set(s) triggered for the SCell is determined according to RRC configuration.<br>  - For early aperiodic CSI reporting, the CSI report configuration(s) triggered for the SCell is determined according to RRC configuration<br>- Alt-2 (Explicit mechanism):<br>  - For early aperiodic SRS-AS transmission, the SRS resource set(s) triggered for the SCell is determined according to an indication in SCell activation command.<br>  - For early aperiodic CSI reporting, the CSI report configuration(s) triggered for the SCell is determined according to an indication in SCell activation command. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2508115](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2508115.zip) |

Assume the receiver matches the transmitter's modulation, i.e., to receiver uses 1SB when transmitter uses 1SB, receiver uses 2SB when transmitter uses 2SB. Companies to report the value, and further down-selection of the values and 2SB/1SB is not precluded. \|<br>\| [2d] \| Modulation \| Modulation \| Companies to report modulation, e.g., OOK, BPSK \|<br>\| [2e] \| Line code \| Line code \| Companies to report, e.g., Manchester encoding, no line coding \|<br>\| [2g] \| FEC \| FEC \| Companies to report, e.g., CC, No FEC \|<br>\| [2h] \| ADC bit width \| ADC bit width \| Companies to report, e.g., 11-bit \|<br>\| [2j] \| D2R receiver \| D2R receiver \| Companies to report, e.g., coherent receiver / non-coherent receiver \|<br>\| [2k] \| Repetition \| Repetition \| Companies to report based on update of Rel-19 if any \|<br>\|  \| Other assumptions \| Other assumptions \| Other assumptions \|<br>\| [3a] \| Other assumptions \| Other assumptions \| To be reported by company \|<br>\| [3b] \| Note: Companies to report required SINR/SNR/CINR/CNR according to BLER target. \| Note: Companies to report required SINR/SNR/CINR/CNR according to BLER target. \| Note: Companies to report required SINR/SNR/CINR/CNR according to BLER target. \| | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2507959](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2507959.zip) |

| Agreement | LLS should be conducted to decide which MCS entries pi/2-BPSK can be extended to.<br>- Both LLS performance gain and power boosting gain due to lower PAPR should be taken into consideration.<br>- Note: LLS performance gain refers to the performance gain/loss when using pi/2-BPSK compared with QPSK with same SE according to LLS; power boosting gain refers to the performance gain acquired due to the low PAPR of pi/2-BPSK compared with QPSK. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2507330](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2507330.zip) |
| Agreement | Pi/2-BPSK is extended to more MCS entries in MCS tables with spectrum efficiency no larger than N (N <= 0.8770).<br>- FFS: value of N | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2507330](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2507330.zip) |
| Agreement | When extending pi/2-BPSK to more MCS entries in MCS tables, the code rate should be doubled to keep spectral efficiency the same. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2507330](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2507330.zip) |
| Agreement | Reuse at least the following definitions and mechanisms in multiple PRACH transmissions with same Tx beam for multiple PRACH transmissions with different Tx beams.<br>- Definition and determination of RO group<br>- definition of time period<br>- SSB-to-RO mapping rule<br>- Note: the terminology RO group stands for the ROs for multiple PRACH transmissions as specified in TS 38.213, i.e., “set consists of  valid PRACH occasions…”. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2507330](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2507330.zip) |
| Agreement | Reuse the definition of time offset between RO groups in multiple PRACH transmissions with same Tx beam for multiple PRACH transmissions with different Tx beams. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2507330](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2507330.zip) |
| Agreement | Reuse the PRACH power control (power ramping between different RACH attempts is FFS) rule of multiple PRACH transmissions with same Tx beam for multiple PRACH transmissions with different Tx beams.<br>- FFS: whether/how power ramping should be supported for multiple PRACH transmissions with different Tx beams. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2507330](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2507330.zip) |
| Agreement | Support indicating the UL beam information via one or a combination of multiple options from following options, for down-selection:<br>- Option 1: implicitly indicated by RA-RNTI<br>- Option 2: explicitly indicated by DCI format 1_0 with CRC scrambled by RA-RNTI (e.g., reserved bits)<br>- Option 3: explicitly indicated by repurposing field(s) in UL Grant in RAR<br>- Option 4: explicitly indicated by introducing new field in MAC RAR<br>- Option 5: explicitly indicated by repurposing bit(s) in MAC RAR<br>FFS: the exact content of UL beam information | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2507330](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2507330.zip) |
| Agreement | For the determination of Tx beam utilization for multiple PRACH transmissions with different Tx beams,<br>- It is up to UE implementation to determine which Tx beams to use, subject to any necessary restriction<br>  - FFS: any necessary restriction<br>- FFS: whether/how gNB can configure UE to use same Tx beams in part of the PRACH transmissions | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2507330](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2507330.zip) |
| Agreement | Reuse the following rules of Msg3 repetition for PUSCH repetition scheduled by DCI 0_0 with C-RNTI.<br>- RV determination rule, including first RV id determination, RV sequence selection, RV cycling, etc.<br>- Available slot determination rule<br>- Only applicable to PUSCH Repetition Type A | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2507330](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2507330.zip) |
| Agreement | Down-select one of the following options to indicate the number of repetitions of PUSCH scheduled by DCI 0_0 with C-RNTI<br>- Option 1: Using at most 2 MSB of MCS field in DCI format 0_0 with CRC scrambled by C-RNTI<br>- Option 2: Based on TDRA<br>- Option 3: Using the padding bits in DCI format 0_0 with CRC scrambled by C-RNTI<br>- Option 4: Using the 2 MSB of HPN field in DCI format 0_0 with CRC scrambled by C-RNTI<br>- Option 5: Using the DAI field in the DCI 1_0 with CRC scrambled by TC-RNTI<br>- Other options are not precluded | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2508116](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2508116.zip) |
| Agreement | Strive for a single mechanism for PUSCH repetition scheduled by DCI 0_0 with C-RNTI. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2508116](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2508116.zip) |
| Agreement | Support [request or capability report] of PUSCH repetition scheduled by DCI 0_0 with C-RNTI at least before receiving RRCReconfiguration via one of the following signalling.<br>- Option 1: Msg3<br>- Option 2: HARQ-ACK of Msg4<br>- FFS: whether to configure a RSRP of DL reference signal for UE to trigger the [request or capability report].<br>FFS: other options and details. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2508116](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2508116.zip) |

| Agreement | The following evaluation parameters are agreed for the evaluation on NR ISAC.<br>\| Parameters \| Assumptions \| Assumptions \|<br>\| BS antenna configuration \| For 4GHz, 4.9GHz: Option 1 as optional Tx: (12,16, 2,1,1;2,16) Rx: (12,16,2,1,1;2,16) Option 2 Tx: (8,8,2,1,1;4,8) Rx: (8,8,2,1,1;4,8) \| For 6GHz: Option 1 as optional Tx: (16,16, 2,1,1;4,16) Rx: (16,16,2,1,1;4,16) Option 2: Tx: (8,8,2,1,1;4,8) Rx: (8,8,2,1,1;4,8) \|<br>\| BS antenna configuration \| Mandatory: = (0.5, 0.8)λ, +45°/-45° polarization Optional: = (0.5, 0.5)λ, +45°/-45° polarization \| Mandatory: = (0.5, 0.8)λ, +45°/-45° polarization Optional: = (0.5, 0.5)λ, +45°/-45° polarization \|<br>- Note: Tx antenna elements and Rx antenna elements are operating simultaneously | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2507423](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2507423.zip) |
| Agreement | The following evaluation parameters are agreed for the evaluation on NR ISAC.<br>\| Parameters \| Assumptions \|<br>\| BS antenna radiation pattern \| Table 9 in Report ITU-R M.2412 \|<br>\| BS antenna mechanic tilt (downtilt angle without electrical tilt) \| 90° in GCS (pointing to horizontal direction) \|<br>\| BS antenna electrical tilt (downtilt angle after mechanic and electrical tilt) \| Option 1: no electrical tilt Option 2: 102° in GCS \|<br>\| Polarized antenna model \| Model-2 in clause 7.3.2 in TR 38.901 \| | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2507423](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2507423.zip) |
| Agreement | The following evaluation parameters are agreed for the evaluation on NR ISAC.<br>\| Parameters \| Assumptions \|<br>\| gNB-target link \| gNB-aerial UE of UMa/RMa parameters in 36.777 with Alternative 3 \|<br>\| Concatenation of TX-target and target-RX links \| Up to company choice between two options for concatenation defined in Step 9 in section 7.9.4.1, TR 38.901 \|<br>\| The power threshold for path dropping after concatenation for target channel \| -25dB and -40dB are respectively used for the two options for concatenation \| | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2507424](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2507424.zip) |

Companies should report how the maximum BS Tx power is derived. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2507424](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2507424.zip) |

Companies to provide details on their modelling. Note: X = -Infinity corresponds to not modelling self-interference \| | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2507425](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2507425.zip) |
| Agreement | For the evaluation on NR ISAC,<br>- 3 kinds of resources are defined<br>- Type 1: resources that are used for sensing signal transmission<br>- Type 2: part of Type 1 resources that are used for communication purpose<br>  - Note: it is possible the resource type 2 doesn’t exist<br>- Type 3: resources that are not used for sensing signal transmission, and cannot be used for communication purpose due to sensing operation<br>- Both options should be reported for sensing resource ratio<br>- Option 1: (Type_1 + Type_3) resources over all radio DL and UL resources<br>- Option 2: (Type_1 - Type_2 + Type_3) resources over all radio DL and UL resources<br>- Note: if Type 2 resource doesn’t exist, two options are the same<br>For the evaluation on NR ISAC,<br>- Company should report T/F RE mapping of sensing RS, assumed TDD UL/DL configuration if applicable.<br>- Company should report which sensing RS resources are considered as Type 2 resource and related reason. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2507426](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2507426.zip) |
| Agreement | Companies are encouraged to report the high-level sensing signal/data processing method used in the evaluation, e.g., 2D FFT, MUSIC, and any other methods. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2507426](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2507426.zip) |
| Agreement | Up to company to model low power cluster in the evaluation of NR ISAC | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2507426](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2507426.zip) |
| Agreement | Companies should report details of the Tx beam information (number of Tx beams, wide/narrow Tx beam) being used at TRP.<br>- The details of the Tx beam information (number of Tx beams, wide/narrow Tx beam) being used at TRP will be captured along with the corresponding evaluation results in the Rel-20 TR | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2507426](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2507426.zip) |
| Agreement | The following performance objectives are adopted for evaluation purpose of NR ISAC.<br>\| Metric \| Value \|<br>\| Missed detection Probability \| [5]% \|<br>\| False Alarm Probability Type 1 \| [5]% \|<br>\| False Alarm Probability Type 2 \| [5]% \|<br>\| Horizontal Positioning Accuracy \| [10] m with confidence level 90% \|<br>\| Vertical Positioning Accuracy \| [10] m with confidence level 90% \|<br>\| Velocity Accuracy \| [5] m/s with confidence level 90% \| | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2507426](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2507426.zip) |

## 10.6.1 NR-NTN GNSS resilience

10 Release 20 NR › 10.6 Non-Terrestrial Networks (NTN) for NR Phase 4

| Agreement | For the evaluation of GNSS resilient NR-NTN operation, consider the following assumptions:<br>- For scenario 1 and 2<br>  - The following time/frequency differences should be evaluated:<br>    - The differential one-way delay (in µs) as calculated between the largest UE-Sat delay and smallest UE sat delay within the uncertainty area.<br>      - For UL performance evaluation 2 times differential one-way delay should be considered.<br>    - The differential one-way Doppler/frequency offset (in ppm) is calculated between the largest UE-sat Doppler/frequency offset and smallest UE-sat Doppler/frequency offset within the uncertainty area.<br>      - For UL performance evaluation, scaled one-way differential Doppler/frequency offset to determine UL differential Doppler/frequency offset should be considered. Scaling factor should be reported. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2506846](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2506846.zip) |

For non-restricted set,<br>      - Note: In practice, the detection performance degrades at frequency offsets exceeding 1 SCS and 2 SCS with restricted set A and B, respectively.<br>      - Othe values of  can be used and reported with justification<br>    - ppm<br>    - is the uplink carrier frequency<br>    - Note: The scaling factor*Differential Doppler is equal to the UL differential Doppler/frequency offset.<br>    - The same scaling factor as per the previous agreement is to be reported<br>Note: the above is applicable to single PRACH transmission. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>[R1-2506848](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Docs/R1-2506848.zip) |

| Agreement | In RAN1#123, based on the following alternatives representing two calculation methodologies, select a calculation methodology with potential adjustments, including correction of equations:<br>Alt 1:<br>For the calculation of the differential one-way delay and the differential one-way Doppler/frequency offset the following could be considered:<br>Geometry model and equations could be considered. ## 11.1 Overview of 6GR air interface

11 Rel-20 Study of 6GR

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | - RAN1 provides methodology and corresponding initial analysis of potentially achievable coverage to RAN#110 to determine the coverage target(s) | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Overall]` |
| Agreement | - High-level aspects to consider for the 6GR sync signal structure include, but not limited to<br>  - Sync raster design<br>  - Spectrum allocation<br>  - smallest maximum supported RF and BB UE BW without spectrum aggregation<br>  - mobile broadband service requirements as high priority<br>  - Energy efficiency for both BS and UE<br>  - Detection/tracking performance, latency, and complexity<br>    - Including initial cell search<br>  - Coverage target<br>  - Common design for diverse device types<br>  - Consideration of the supported deployment<br>  - Consideration on whether the single sync signal structure is sufficient<br>  - Note: Aspects impacting on the periodicity is to be discussed under AI11.5 | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Overall]` |
| Agreement | - The aspects to consider for supporting NTN include, but not limited to<br>  - Initial access, including cell search and SSB periodicity<br>  - Coverage<br>  - Duplexing<br>  - Capacity<br>  - Signalling overhead<br>  - GNSS-less/resilient/based operation<br>  - Large/varying doppler and propagation delay<br>  - Beamforming / beam management / beam hopping<br>Note:<br>- High-level aspects to consider to enable lower CAPEX/OPEX with respect to current networks include, but not limited to<br>  - UE/NW implementation complexity<br>  - UE/NW energy efficiency<br>  - MRSS<br>  - Spectrum efficiency | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Overall]` |

| Conclusion | The following existing traffic models could be used for 6GR performance evaluations,<br>- Full buffer<br>- FTP Model 1 (in TR 36.814)<br>- FTP Model 3 (in TR 36.872)<br>- XR Traffic models (in TR 38.838)<br>- VoIP model (as in TR 36.814)<br>- Instant message (as in TR 38.840)<br>- Note that which model(s) will be used can be further decided when performing simulations in each individual topic. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Evaluation]` |
| Agreement | For the study traffic model(s) for 6GR AI/ML services:<br>  - A representative AI/ML service is the generative AI, e.g., as defined in TR22.870.<br>Send LS to SA4 (cc RAN2, SA1, SA2) requesting input if any on traffic characteristics for AI/ML services.<br>Note: RAN1 is discussing the following options for the model:<br>  - Option-1a: The model is parameterized by Token, e.g., Token size, Token arrival rate, and Token delay budget.<br>    - Token is the minimum unit of data generated in the application layer.<br>    - How to associate Tokens to PHY layer packets.<br>    - How to reflect the variable importance of tokens.<br>    - Whether other parameters are additionally needed when tokens are encapsulated together into a packet, e.g., packet arrival rate, packet success rate, and packet delay.<br>  - Option-1b: The model is characterized by the parameters of PHY layer packet, including e.g., packet size, arrival rates, latency requirement, reliability requirement, etc.<br>  - Option-1c: reusing or extending the FTP-3/XR traffic model.<br>  - FFS other models/options need to be defined for other AI/ML services. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Evaluation]` |
| Agreement | Study traffic modelling for evaluations related to immersive communication services including but not limited to advanced XR [e.g., TR22.870] and haptics services,<br>- XR traffic models (in TR 38.838) are considered as starting point.<br>  - FFS the detailed modifications on the parameters to the XR traffic model, e.g., higher packet size, higher packet arrival rate, higher packet size deviation, PDB, etc.<br>- FFS how many models need to be defined and the corresponding representative use cases.<br>- FFS how to incorporate haptics traffic (TR26.854).<br>Send LS to SA4 requesting input if any on the relevant traffic characteristics, RAN1 can continue the study before SA4 potential response. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Evaluation]` |
| Agreement | Study extensions to FTP Model 1/FTP Model 3 to incorporate the following:<br>- Multiple packet sizes and associated time-domain behaviors (e.g., inter arrival time)<br>  - FFS number of packet sizes (e.g., 2 or 3).<br>  - FFS whether to have fixed or variable packet size and packet arrival rate for a given UE.<br>  - FFS applicability of multiple packet sizes to only one or both of FTP Model 1/FTP Model 3.<br>  - FFS packet size and arrival rate characteristics.<br>- Packet delay budget (PDB) related parameters<br>  - FFS PDB applicability to packets (e.g., one PDB parameter for only one traffic flow or different PDB parameters for different traffic flows).<br>  - FFS how to consider the PDB, e.g., whether to drop packets when exceeding the budget, PDB aware metric.<br>- Note consider the following for PDB:<br>  - Applicability to the extension to FTP Model 1/ FTP Model 3 with one packet size.<br>  - Applicability or not to the extension to FTP Model 1/ FTP Model 3 with multiple packet sizes. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Evaluation]` |

## 11.3.1 Waveform

11 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 11.3 Waveform and frame structure for 6GR air interface

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | Draft LS R1-2508068 is endorsed with following revision:<br>- removing “Additionally, if time permits, any feedback for CP-OFDM PAPR reduction/MPR values achievable by implementation is also appreciated.” | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Waveform]` |
| Agreement | Final LS R1-2508069 is endorsed. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Waveform]` |
| Agreement | - For uplink low-PAPR proposals, the link level performance evaluation criterion is Net Gain assuming same spectrum efficiency as the reference<br>  - Net Gain [dB] = Tx power gain relative to the reference – SNR degradation relative to the reference @10% BLER<br>    - A realistic PA model should be used<br>    - When calculating the Tx power gain, the RAN4 metrics on the Tx power should be taken into account.<br>    - For SNR degradation, fading channel and non-ideal channel estimation, including DMRS configuration, and equalization is encouraged.<br>    - FFS: Other evaluation metrics<br>    - Note: Companies to report how to calculate the Tx power gain, modulation and coding | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Waveform]` |
| Agreement | - Study the evaluation method for evaluating DFT-s-OFDM for UL with number of layers > 1. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Waveform]` |

## 11.3.2 Frame structure

11 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 11.3 Waveform and frame structure for 6GR air interface

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | For communication, 6GR considers NR frame structure used as a starting point for the study item,<br>  - Resource defined by one subcarrier and one symbol is called as resource element (RE).<br>  - Resource block (RB) is defined where the number of consecutive subcarriers per RB is the same for all numerologies and the number of subcarriers per RB is 12<br>  - Radio Frame length is 10ms<br>  - Each radio frame is split into 10 subframes, each with a duration of 1 ms<br>  - For given SCS and for given symbol, the symbol duration, normal CP length and boundary is same as NR design.<br>  - A slot is defined as supporting 14 consecutive symbols for normal CP case and all subcarrier spacings. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Frame structure]` |
| Agreement | 6GR study assumes same SCS between 6GR Sync signals and other channels/signals (except PRACH) for a given band.<br>- FFS: same/different SCS between 6GR sync signal and other channels/signals (except PRACH) for FR2-1.<br>- Note: ISAC is separately discussed in ISAC session. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Frame structure]` |
| Agreement | - RAN1 assumes 400MHz maximum channel bandwidth at network side and 30kHz SCS around 7GHz<br>- Study whether and how to enable UE to support 400MHz bandwidth | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Frame structure]` |

## 11.4.1 Channel coding

11 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 11.4 Channel coding and modulation for 6GR interface

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Working Assumption | - Study 6G data channel coding for higher throughput than 5G with acceptable performance-complexity tradeoff for both NW side and UE side,<br>  - Target peak data rate is assumed to be 2 times of the target peak data rate defined in TR38.913<br>Note: The other target throughput is up to company to report.<br>Note: Applicability of the potential channel code will be further discussed. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |
| Agreement | - For 6G channel coding, LDPC is used for data (including SIBs) and Polar code is used for L1 control information (larger than 11 bits, including PBCH)<br>- For 6G LDPC<br>  - Working assumption: For data rate within NR range, reuse of NR LDPC design is supported<br>  - For data rate beyond NR range, study LDPC extension with acceptable performance-complexity tradeoff for both NW side and UE side<br>    - Note: Applicability of the potential LDPC extension to data rate within NR range will be further discussed<br>- For 6G Polar code<br>  - Working assumption: For control information within NR range (larger than 11 bits), reuse of NR Polar code design is supported<br>  - For control information beyond NR range, study Polar code extension with acceptable performance-complexity tradeoff for both NW side and UE side<br>    - Note: Necessity for control information beyond NR range is to be further discussed<br>    - Polar code maximum mother code length is kept as 1024.<br>  - FFS: further motivation(s) for potential extension/enhancement until RAN1#123 | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Channel coding]` |

## 11.4.2 Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation

11 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 11.4 Channel coding and modulation for 6GR interface

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | For 6GR constellation shaping evaluation for CP-OFDM, and improved MCS table, the proposed scheme will be compared with non-shaping with NR MCS table. The evaluation and comparison should consider at least the following:<br>- BLER performance under AWGN channel (at least for performance calibration)<br>  - 1st transmission (baseline) and with HARQ re-transmission<br>- BLER performance under fading channel with fixed MCS<br>  - 1st transmission (baseline) and with HARQ re-transmission<br>- Throughput performance with link adaptation (adaptive MCS and rank) under fading channel<br>  - Needs to provide assumptions on rate adaptation (e.g., target BLER for 1st transmission, maximum # of retransmissions)<br>- Transmitter and receiver complexity (e.g., shaping/deshaping, demapper), latency, parallelism implementation, and storage requirements,<br>- Other KPI not excluded, such as PAPR, EVM, MPR/A-MPR<br>- Expected spec impact<br>- FFS detailed assumption of constellation shaping and improved MCS table<br>- System level evaluation can be done after link level evaluation. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |
| Agreement | For 6GR constellation shaping study, proponent is encouraged to provide details for the PS/GS schemes considered for evaluation and comparison, including at least the following<br>- Probabilistic shaping for CP-OFDM and DFT-s-OFDM<br>  - Use the list of spectrum efficiencies in NR MCS table as starting point, and provide constellation (including normalization), coding rate and target probabilistic distribution for each SE<br>    - If multiple coding rate and target probabilistic distribution pairs are provided for each SE, how to switch between them<br>  - Relationship between shaping and FEC, coded bits to modulation symbol mapping, and other modules (such as scrambling, interleaving), in transmit and receive chains. How to handle HARQ retransmission<br>  - PS algorithm details (for example, source coding based, channel coding based, etc) and parameters (such as block length, rate loss)<br>- Geometric shaping for CP-OFDM and DFT-s-OFDM<br>  - Use the list of spectrum efficiencies in NR MCS table as starting point, and provide target constellation shape (including normalization) (1D-NUC, 2D-NUC, QAM-CS, etc) for each SE<br>    - If multiple constellation shapes are provided for each SE, how to switch between them<br>  - GS mapping details, such as bit to constellation point mapping and shape<br>  - Relationship with other blocks (such as scrambling, interleaving). How to handle HARQ retransmission | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |
| Agreement | For link level simulation for modulation evaluation, companies are encouraged to evaluate with the following assumptions and should report the exact scheme evaluated.<br>- channel configuration, including Channel profiles,Tx/RX antenna settings<br>- For MIMO scenario: SU-MIMO or MU-MIMO, follow agenda item 11.2 for MIMO when available.<br>- Precoder assumption<br>  - Close loop MIMO (reciprocal beamforming (e.g., SVD, SLR/RZF, etc.), codebook based)<br>    - Realistic CSI/SRS/AP-SRS periodicity and delay, and SRS chanEst assumptions,<br>    - or genie beamforming<br>  - Open loop MIMO<br>- Receiver assumption (for MIMO): LMMSE (baseline) for UL, rML or LMMSE for DL<br>- LLR demapper: Max-log (baseline) or Log-MAP<br>- Channel estimation: Realistic (baseline) or ideal<br>- Other assumptions: Channel coding NR-LDPC (baseline), PxSCH bandwidth, SCS, FD interleaver used or not, 5GNR BICM interleaver usage<br>- Note: For MIMO, SIMO, MISO and SISO are included when possible | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |
| Agreement | For 6GR constellation shaping evaluation for DFT-s-OFDM, and improved MCS table, the proposed scheme will be compared with non-shaping with NR MCS table. In addition to what has been agreed for CP-OFDM in earlier agreement, the evaluation and comparison should further consider at least the following:<br>- PAPR/CM of the resulting waveform<br>- EVM, MPR/A-MPR | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |
| Agreement | For the study of uniform 4096QAM for DL and uniform 1024QAM for UL, need to study performance (assuming realistic channel estimation, time/freq synchronization assumption, phase noise assumption, etc), complexity/power consumption, requirements, benefit/necessity under applicable scenarios, associated restrictions, and challenges (such as EVM requirement, PAPR increase, MPR or A-MPR increase under realistic PA model).<br>- FFS: How to involve RAN4 early<br>- FFS: Shaping of higher order modulation<br>- System level evaluation can be done after link level evaluation. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation]` |

## 11.5 Energy efficiency

11 Rel-20 Study of 6GR

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | At least the following NR metrics,<br>- Network energy saving gain relative to baseline for BS<br>- UE energy saving gain relative to baseline for UE<br>- Impact to UPT (User-Perceived Throughput), if applicable,<br>as well as the metrics<br>- Impact to latency, if applicable<br>- Impact to QoS/delay budget satisfaction rate, if applicable<br>are used for 6G energy efficiency evaluation. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| Agreement | Apply the following evaluation methodology framework for Quantitative analysis,<br>- For NW unloaded/empty load case or UE idle/inactive mode:<br>- For energy saving: analytical calculation<br>- For performance impact: analytical calculation, LLS<br>- For loaded cases and connected-mode UEs<br>- For energy saving: SLS<br>- For performance impact: LLS, SLS | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| Agreement | For evaluation purposes, expand the existing BS power model reference configuration with a set for ~7 GHz operation with the following parameters:<br>\| Property \| Configuration for Set 4 around 7 GHz \|<br>\| Duplex \| TDD \|<br>\| BW \| [100, 200, 400] MHz \|<br>\| SCS \| [30 kHz, 60 kHz] \|<br>\| Number of TRP \| 1 \|<br>\| Total number of DL TX RUs \| [128, 256] \|<br>\| Total DL power level \| [56] dBm \|<br>\| Total number of UL Rx RUs \| [128, 256] \|<br>Note: Bracketed values to be confirmed. Other values are not precluded.<br>The above configuration has no implication on supported BW, SCS for 6GR. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| Agreement | Study whether/how to further update the BS model considering the following aspects, e.g.,<br>- Whether to downselect between Cat.1 and Cat. 2,<br>- Updates of parameter values (including defining a new Cat),<br>- Updates of power scaling, power states (including additional PSs)<br>- Etc.<br>Note: The defined BS power models does not preclude use case-specific enhancements regarding, e.g., multi-TRP, SBFD, multi-carrier etc | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| Agreement | Study and evaluate NW energy savings and the impact on UE performance and user experience with respect to 20ms and longer periodicities of sync signal(s) at least for initial access with the following consideration, but not limited to:<br>BS assumptions:<br>- Cell-common signaling (e.g., sync signal(s), broadcast PDCCH, SIB-1, SIB, paging, PRACH), e.g.,<br>  - Clustered provisioning of different cell-common signaling,<br>  - On-demand provisioning of different cell-common signaling,<br>- UE-specific signaling (for low, light, medium loads), e.g.,<br>  - Clustered provisioning with cell-common signaling,<br>  - Unclustered provisioning with cell-common signaling,<br>UE impact:<br>- Cell search complexity and latency, including frequency search latency,<br>- UE Power consumption,<br>- Sync signal detection, coverage and tracking performance,<br>- RRM, mobility,<br>- Beam management,<br>- Other properties are not precluded,<br>- Improvements to address identified impact, e.g.,<br>  - Additional sync signal needs,<br>  - Adaptation of sync signal transmission periodicity,<br>  - Sparser synch raster. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| Agreement | Study and evaluate on-demand and/or periodic SIB-1 transmission with respect to<br>- NW energy savings potential and UE power consumption impact,<br>- SIB-1 acquisition delay,<br>- NW and UE complexity,<br>- Coverage,<br>- Applicable deployment scenarios, e.g.,:<br>  - Standalone cell/carrier,<br>  - Multiple TRPs/cells/carriers. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| Agreement | For 6GR energy efficiency evaluation purposes, reuse the existing UE power consumption model FR1 and FR2 reference configurations in TR 38.840 for operation up to around 7GHz and within 24.25 GHz – 52.6 GHz, respectively.<br>- Scaling rules can be updated, including additional rule(s) for scaling UE power consumption, and including around 7GHz specific update<br>  - FFS: details.<br>- Power value and transition time update, if necessary, including around 7GHz specific update<br>- No implication on supported BW, SCS, modulation and antenna setting for 6GR<br>- Revisit if SCS for around 7GHz is different with respect to the reference configuration | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| Agreement | Study and evaluate DL WUS of OFDM based sequence and corresponding mechanisms for 6GR EE improvement, regarding at least the following aspects:<br>- Coverage target for DL WUS (e.g., same as PDCCH, common sync signal, or other)<br>- Measurements and/or synchronization.<br>- System overhead and network energy consumption/UE energy saving for UE operation with the DL WUS.<br>- RRC states<br>- Other functionalities | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| Agreement | For evaluation purposes, study extending NR UE power consumption scaling w.r.t. at least BW and/or antenna setting, considering at least the different characteristics in RF/BB power consumption and static/dynamic power consumption. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| Agreement | For evaluation purposes, study extending NR UE power consumption model for UE operation with DL WUS of OFDM-based sequence, regarding the following aspects:<br>- Power state(s), sleep and non-sleep, and corresponding characteristics and power value(s)<br>- Transition energy and time for each of sleep state(s)<br>- Companies to report the assumption(s) for achieving the proposed power value(s), e.g., time/frequency domain detection, noise figure assumption(s), synchronization assumption(s), BW/antenna assumption(s), etc. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| Agreement | Study and evaluate on-demand sync signal(s) mechanisms for 6GR energy efficiency, considering, e.g.,:<br>- On-demand sync signal(s) for single cell/carrier, multi-carrier/cell, multi-TRP,<br>- Network-triggered and UE-triggered on-demand sync signal(s),<br>- Idle and/or connected modes,<br>- Other mechanisms/aspects/signals/channels are not precluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
| Agreement | Study and evaluate multi-carrier/cells/TRPs mechanisms for 6GR NES, considering, e.g.,:<br>- Sync signal-less carriers/cells/TRPs for at least intra-band and collocated inter-band multi-carrier/cell/TRPs, including potential extensions to additional deployments and scenarios,<br>- RRC states,<br>- UE energy consumption and complexity,<br>- Other mechanisms/aspects/signals/channels are not precluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#122b eom4](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122b/Inbox/Chair_Notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122b%20eom4.docx)<br>`[122bis-R20-6GR-Energy efficiency]` |
